## 2. Related Work

Autoformulation and evaluation. NL4Opt (Ramamonjison et a<sup>l</sup>., 2022) studies <sup>f</sup>ormu<sup>l</sup>ation. OptiMUS and O<sub>p</sub>tiMUS-0.3 build modular formulation and execution <sub>p</sub>i<sub>p</sub>elines (AhmadiTeshnizi et al., 2024b,a), while O<sub>p</sub>tiMind adds ex<sub>p</sub>ert-<sub>g</sub>uided trainin<sub>g</sub> and inference (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025). ORLM, LLMOPT and ORThou<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>g</sub>enerate and revise mathematical models, code and solver out<sub>p</sub>uts (Huan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025a; Jian<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025; Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026). MAMO, O<sub>p</sub>tiBench and OPT En<sub>g</sub>ine broaden evaluation tasks (Huan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025b; Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025; Chen et al., 2026). Feasibilit<sub>y</sub> and reference objectives <sub>assess</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>answers,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t d<sub>o no</sub>t <sub>es</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bli<sub>s</sub>h th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e wr</sub>itt<sub>en mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>,</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>an</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>co</sub>d<sub>e an</sub>d <sub>repor</sub>t d<sub>escr</sub>ib<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e same op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on pro</sub>bl<sub>em.</sub>

Verification and repair. ReLoop tests program <sup>b</sup>e<sup>h</sup>avior under parameter c<sup>h</sup>anges (Lian et a<sup>l</sup>., 2026). O<sub>p</sub>t-Verifier jud<sub>g</sub>es structure and solutions (Liu et al., 2026), and SAC O<sub>p</sub>t uses semantic anchors for revision (Zhan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026). E<sub>q</sub>uivaMa<sub>p</sub> and ORGEval com<sub>p</sub>are variable ma<sub>pp</sub>in<sub>g</sub>s or <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h structure (Zhai et al., 2025; Wan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025). Falsification uses solver-based testin<sub>g</sub> under controlled instance transformations, whereas FLARE verifies MILP reformulations usin<sub>g</sub> theorem <sub>p</sub>rovin<sub>g</sub> (Li and Hai, 2026; Robbins et al., 2026). ORSMT re<sub>p</sub>airs instance-s<sub>p</sub>ecific mathematical, executable and re<sub>p</sub>ort artifacts. Therefore<sub>,</sub> re<sub>p</sub>air com<sub>p</sub>arisons use ReLoo<sub>p</sub> and O<sub>p</sub>t-Verifier.

Formal semantic verification. Denotationa<sup>l</sup> tests eva<sup>l</sup>uate program <sup>b</sup>e<sup>h</sup>avior <sup>b</sup>eyond textua<sup>l</sup> similarit<sub>y</sub> (Zhon<sub>g</sub> et al., 2020). Z3 and cvc5 decide arithmetic formulas (de Moura and Bjørner, 2008; Barbosa et al., 2022), while neural s<sub>y</sub>mbolic methods translate <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osed statements into formal <sub>q</sub>ueries (Jha et al., 2023; Pan et al., 2023; Ye et al., 2023; Olausson et al., 2023). ORSMT verifies source requirements, feasible regions, objective preferences and reports in this way. Its evidence id<sub>en</sub>tifi<sub>es m</sub>i<sub>ss</sub>i<sub>ng, a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d <sub>re</sub>d<sub>un</sub>d<sub>an</sub>t <sub>res</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons an</sub>d <sub>suppor</sub>t<sub>s repa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> f<sub>rom e</sub>ith<sub>er usa</sub>bl<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>.</sub>

(1). A contradiction, t<sub>yp</sub>e mismatch, or unmatched observable d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on preven</sub>t<sub>s a syn</sub>t<sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>c correspon</sub>d<sub>ence</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> b<sub>e</sub>i<sub>ng accep</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d l<sub>eaves</sub> th<sub>e correspon</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> <sub>re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> U<sub>nde</sub>c<sub>ided.</sub> Thi<sub>s separa</sub>ti<sub>on a</sub>ll<sub>ows seman</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y equ</sub>i<sub>va</sub>l<sub>en</sub>t <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o use</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t <sub>aux</sub>ili<sub>ary</sub> <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>ons or re</sub>d<sub>un</sub>d<sub>an</sub>t <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ng a one-</sub>t<sub>o-one ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h <sub>o</sub>f <sub>a</sub>ll <sub>so</sub>l<sub>ver var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>es.</sub>

Target and normalization. ORSMT supports reconstruction in <sup>b</sup>ot<sup>h</sup> directions. W<sup>h</sup>en t<sup>h</sup>e mat<sup>h</sup>e-<sub>ma</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l � i<sub>s ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e,</sub> ORSMT <sub>uses</sub> it <sub>as</sub> th<sub>e pr</sub>i<sub>mary cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>; o</sub>th<sub>erw</sub>i<sub>se,</sub> it <sub>uses</sub> the captured executable model � = H (�b) as the target for reconstructing missing mathematical artifacts. Before construction, ORSMT a<sub>pp</sub>lies a normalization o<sub>p</sub>erator (N) that <sub>p</sub>reserves the <sub>p</sub>rojected feasible region and objective graph relevant to the observable decisions. The normalization <sub>may ma</sub>k<sub>e</sub> i<sub>mp</sub>li<sub>c</sub>it d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>ns exp</sub>li<sub>c</sub>it <sub>an</sub>d d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ve re</sub>d<sub>un</sub>d<sub>an</sub>t <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s or aux</sub>ili<sub>ary</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>n</sub>iti<sub>ons nee</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d for compilation, while preserving the semantic model objects used for verification. The resulting <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>en use</sub>d <sub>as</sub> th<sub>e cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Mathematics to program. For t<sup>h</sup>e mat<sup>h</sup>ematica<sup>l</sup> target $T = A _ { . }$ <sub>,</sub> ORSMT com<sub>p</sub>utes

$$
\widetilde { A } = T , \qquad \widetilde { P } = \mathrm { C o m p i l e } ( N ( T ) ) , \qquad \widetilde { B } = \mathcal { H } ( \widetilde { P } ) , \qquad \widetilde { R } = \mathrm { S o l v e } ( \widetilde { P } ) .\tag{3}

F.4. An incorrect Opt-Verifier judgment on a certified solution
<table><tr><td colspan="2">BWOR 18: a correct result receives incorrect revision advice</td></tr><tr><td>Returned assignment. The companies choose stores 3, 2, 1, 4 and 5. The</td><td>Verifier comment.</td></tr><tr><td>costs are 7, 9, 6, 6 and 6, giving 34 in total. Formal checks.</td><td>The proposed alternative uses stores 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5. Its listed costs are 4, 9, 12, 6 and 6. The comment labels this total as 34.</td></tr><tr><td>The model pair is consistent. The decision is fea- sible, its value is bound correctly, and no better feasible decision exists in the compared models.</td><td>Arithmetic check. The listed alternative costs total 37. The alternative supplies no improvement over the certified value 34.</td></tr></table>

BWOR <sub>p</sub>roblem 18 at 14B assi<sub>g</sub>ns five construction com<sub>p</sub>anies to five stores. Its recorded model contains 25 variables and ten constraints. Both region inclusions, objective coeficients and preferences agree. The reported objective 34 matches the reference value, and the recorded exact checks certify both region inclusions, equal objective coeficients, equal decision preferences, feasibility under both models and objective binding. The query for a better feasible decision is unsatisfiable.

The Opt-Verifier reproduction accepts the structural comparison but returns a negative solution judgment. Its proposed alternative has store indices (1, 2, 3, 4, 5) and costs (4, 9, 12, 6, 6), which sum to 37 rather than the claimed 34. The returned assignment (3, 2, 1, 4, 5) instead costs $( 7 + 9 + 6 + 6 + 6 = 3 4 )$ Th<sub>e error</sub> i<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ee</sub>db<sub>ac</sub>k <sub>ar</sub>ith<sub>me</sub>ti<sub>c, no</sub>t <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>on or var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e a</sub>li<sub>gnmen</sub>t<sub>.</sub>
